# Support matrix

Evidence through: 2026-10-26. This is the sole live implementation/validation status record. Dated evidence below retains the limits of each tested slice; it is not a claim that documentation edits reran those checks. Repository milestone history and source-release metadata are in the [changelog](../../CHANGELOG.md); publication records are in [GitHub Releases](https://github.com/animu-sphere/tessera/releases). No install/export package or prebuilt binary is distributed.

## Evidence vocabulary

| Status | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| Planned | Design/roadmap direction; no implemented capability |
| Implemented | Identifiable code exists; validation scope must be stated separately |
| Validated | A recorded configuration and reproducible check establish the specific claim |
| Unsupported | An implementation explicitly rejects or excludes a declared configuration |

Missing implementation or test evidence is not evidence that a platform fails. Use planned/unvalidated rather than inventing support results.

## Capability status

Each row names the status and the scope of the claim, then links the dated evidence that establishes it; details and limits stay in those evidence sections. A capability without a row has no implementation: its scope is in [current](../roadmap/current.md) or [backlog](../roadmap/backlog.md), with design constraints in architecture and possible mechanisms in proposals. No CLI, RPC/MCP transport, WebGPU or browser host, or snapshot bundle exists.

## Configuration status

| Configuration | Evidence |
| --- | --- |
| Core-only build | Windows x64/MSVC Debug library-only build at [menu acceptance](#v020-menu-acceptance--2026-10-10); 57 core, example, and independent source-consumer checks verified in Debug and Release at [first-app onboarding](#first-app-onboarding-evidence--2026-10-17), reusing the fresh configuration at [form authoring](#c-form-authoring-evidence--2026-10-16); no external runtime dependencies |
| Vulkan | SDK/Slang artifacts, offscreen GPU runtime fixtures, and a Win32 FIFO swapchain menu smoke verified below |
| Fonts module | HarfBuzz 10.2.0 / FreeType 2.13.3 from vcpkg classic mode, `x64-windows`, Windows x64/MSVC Debug and Release shaping, family/alias selection, grayscale raster/cache and combined Vulkan atlas image fixtures verified below; other acquisitions, triplets, and platforms unvalidated |
| Windows / Linux / macOS | Windows x64/MSVC Debug/Release core and combined font/Vulkan checks, and Win32 menu smoke and physical mouse/controller sessions verified below; Linux/macOS and other Windows toolchains unvalidated |

Core, offscreen Vulkan, and Win32 host evidence below are distinct. Physical-device evidence is limited to one mouse, one USB DualSense, and one original Bluetooth Joy-Con L/R pair on this machine. Real-text offscreen and native menu images use the declared Noto fixtures on one configuration; no evidence establishes packaging or broad Windows support.

## Renderer ambient-strength evidence — 2026-10-26

- Revision/scope: local changes on base `3bca1d4d0415da7e9adfcb2a02761898502565ad`. [Numeric composition](../../examples/renderer-diagnostics/panel.hpp), [host](../../examples/renderer-diagnostics/host.cpp), [copied main](../../examples/renderer-diagnostics/main.cpp), [fixture](../../tests/integrations/renderer_diagnostics_tests.cpp) and [guide](../guides/renderer-diagnostics.md#edit-ambient-strength-independently). The [Implemented consumer owner](../integrations/renderer-diagnostics.md#implemented-ambient-strength-control-boundary) defines independent numeric lifetimes, explicit field routing, draft/setting commands and publication. No core API, serialized field, semantic role or optional dependency changes.
- Configuration: existing core-only `build-v040-authoring`, Windows x64, Visual Studio 18 2026, MSVC 19.51.36256.0, MSBuild 18.9.1+a81b43525, Windows SDK 10.0.26100.0, CMake/CTest 4.4.3; tests/examples ON, fonts/Vulkan OFF, no dependency toolchain or download. Full Debug/Release builds used `cmake --build build-v040-authoring --config <configuration> --parallel 2 -- /nodeReuse:false`; final fixture-only builds used the same command with `--target tessera_renderer_diagnostics_tests`. Aggregate, final and copied build logs contain no compiler warnings. All 28 copied-file SHA-256 hashes match the seven reusable files across both imports/configurations.
- Procedure/results: selected checks used `ctest --test-dir build-v040-authoring -C <configuration> -R '^(renderer_diagnostics.*|settings|authoring|text_edit|event|semantics|semantic_snapshot|layout|layout_contract|scroll|focus|pointer|commands|style_sheet|theme|paint|inspection|cpu_renderer|replay|offscreen_runner)$' --output-on-failure --no-tests=error --output-log C:/dev/tessera/build-v040-authoring/renderer-ambient-<configuration-lowercase>.log -j 2`. Release passed all 23; Debug passed the other 22 and the final contract passed separately with `-R '^renderer_diagnostics_contract$'` after correcting a new test's assumption that presentational TextInput children expose separate semantic nodes. The final Release contract executable also exited zero through direct execution. Thus all 23 affected checks, including both independent copied imports, have passing evidence in both configurations; final observation assertions ran in both. Documentation/link/ownership checks and `git diff --check` passed. No optional backend/native checks were rerun.
- Operations/observations: the numeric scenario runs independently for both fields, with an incomplete reverse-selected peer draft retained throughout. Each reports ten accepted-view CPU/replay comparisons; direct reports 43 setting attempts/21 accepted changes and ambient 44/22, with three intentional outside/unmapped camera deliveries each. The additional two-field scenario reports six comparisons and zero camera deliveries. Telemetry reports 38 comparisons; MSAA and both Boolean scenarios also pass. The copied main prints direct strength/draft 1.5, ambient 0.75, MSAA 8/four supported choices, outlines on and scene lights off, with 12 setting attempts, ten accepted changes and zero camera deliveries.
- Retention/rejection: different incomplete drafts and reverse selections survive A/B/C refresh, MSAA/capabilities, both Boolean toggle/set operations, independent numeric disable/re-enable and controls collapse/re-expansion. Both fields preserve their peer through editing, Apply, Cancel and equal-value typed sets. Every numeric input/operation owner recovers/reveals the controls header on collapse. Equal revisions do not authorize cross-field owners; stale revisions, foreign equal-counter presentations, wrong types/fields/schema, unknown setting identities, hidden/disabled targets, invalid decimal drafts, canonical byte overflow and duplicate sequences reject. Entry/adjustment parity, lower/upper clamping, dirty-draft eligibility, equal Apply/set revision/accounting and Cancel without a setting attempt are covered.
- Failure/arbitration: injected measurement/paint failures in numeric editing, Apply, Cancel, adjustment and ambient eligibility retain both fields, unrelated settings, disclosure, accepted command generation, focus/offset, document/styles, inspection, geometry and paint. Numeric diagnostics preserve field and preparation-source paths within the command bound. Entered failed commands consume their sequence and fresh requests retry once. Consumed invalid Apply/edit failures, disabled pointer hits and claimed streams through replacement/release/cancel cannot deliver to the synthetic camera or execute a second logical action. Host execution/consumption assertions are separate from accepted-view reconstruction. Inspection properties and the input owner's aggregated semantic name expose the exact accepted draft/selection for both fields; repeated semantics/layout/paint and CPU/offscreen replay bytes agree.
- Execution boundary/artifacts/limits: initial sandboxed builds/focused execution progressed, while a later CTest child launch stalled and was interrupted; complete builds, copied imports and final checks ran outside the restricted sandbox. Logs use `renderer-ambient-` under ignored `build-v040-authoring`; final contract output is retained separately, and copied configure/build/test logs live under `source-consumers/renderer-diagnostics/<import>/<configuration>`. Images remain in memory at the fixed 720×160 logical viewport, scale 1.25, locale `en`, placeholder shaping and synthetic scalar glyphs. This is synchronous synthetic-host evidence, without renderer acknowledgement, physical-device input, real-font/GPU panel capture, a generalized numeric/input framework or performance claims.

## Premultiplied image alpha evidence — 2026-10-25

- Revision/scope: local changes on base `ab8cf72010226ebe7bf2d2003a1048ed0f9c1100`. `ImageAlpha` in [draw_list.hpp](../../include/tessera/render/draw_list.hpp), the Vulkan [renderer](../../backends/vulkan/renderer.cpp), [shader](../../backends/vulkan/shaders/primitive.slang) and [header](../../backends/vulkan/include/tessera/vulkan/renderer.hpp), the scalar [CPU renderer](../../src/render/cpu_renderer.cpp), [CPU fixtures](../../tests/render/cpu_renderer_tests.cpp) and [Vulkan comparison fixtures](../../tests/render/vulkan_tests.cpp). The [rendering contract](../architecture/rendering.md#implemented-vulkan-primitive-boundary) owns binding, blending, tint and batching rules. The consumer is hydra-toon's GPU handoff, whose linear premultiplied RGBA8 product is bound without unpremultiplication (HOST-Q2 of the GPU handoff policy in [hydra-toon's documentation](https://github.com/animu-sphere/hydra-toon/tree/main/docs)). No serialized field, draw-list command or core-only behaviour changes; straight alpha stays the default.
- Configuration: `build-v040-premultiplied`, Windows x64, Visual Studio 18 2026, MSVC 19.51.36256.0, Windows SDK 10.0.26100.0, CMake/CTest 4.4.3; Vulkan ON and examples ON, fonts OFF, Vulkan SDK 1.4.350.0 and Slang 2026.8. GPU: NVIDIA RTX A5000, vendor 4318, driver value 2504261632, Vulkan 1.4.329, RGBA8 sRGB target, required Khronos validation and synchronization validation.
- Procedure: [optional Vulkan workflow](../guides/development.md#optional-vulkan-workflow) with the directory above; `ctest --test-dir build-v040-premultiplied -C <configuration> --output-on-failure -E vulkan_menu_smoke` in Debug and Release, then `ctest ... -R '^vulkan$' -V` after the final fixture edit in both configurations. All four SPIR-V artifacts passed `spirv-val --target-env vulkan1.1`. Documentation checks use `scripts/check-docs.ps1`; whitespace checks use `git diff --check`.
- CPU fixtures: a 1×1 linear UNORM premultiplied texel `(64,32,0,128)` over opaque white yields `(224,207,187,255)`, so its RGB is not weighted by alpha again; under tint alpha 0.5 over transparent it yields `(99,71,0,64)`, so tint alpha scales premultiplied RGB. An unknown alpha value rejects with `unknown_value` at `/image/alpha` on both backends.
- GPU comparison: the existing mixed primitive/image/synthetic-glyph scene is unchanged, and its maximum errors by scale, 0.00736163, 0.00658295, 0.00705586 and 0.00628694 in both paths and configurations, equal the [recorded values](#scalar-cpu-rendering-evidence--2026-10-12), so straight-alpha pixels are unchanged. A separate 32×32 logical scene draws a 2×2 linear UNORM premultiplied texture with partial and zero coverage, untinted and tinted `(1,0.8,1,0.6)`, over an opaque background, at scales 1, 1.25, 1.5 and 2 on both paths: maximum errors 0.00671099, 0.00736163, 0.00767107 and 0.0074938, each one sRGB step, within 2/255. Adding the premultiplied images to the mixed scene instead overlapped its rotated and glyph edges and exceeded the tolerance by one or two sRGB steps at bright pixels, so the scenes stay separate. Zero validation errors.
- Result: all 67 CTest targets other than `vulkan_menu_smoke` passed in Debug and Release, including the CPU fixtures, both source-consumer imports with and without Vulkan, and `vulkan`. Debug, Release and the menu example built without warnings.
- Limits/artifacts: `cpu-compare-premultiplied-{reference,batch}-{1,2,3,4}.ppm` beside the existing comparison images under `build-v040-premultiplied/backends/vulkan/artifacts`; GPU logs `premultiplied-gpu-{debug,release}.log` and the Release CTest log `premultiplied-release.log` under the ignored build directory. `vulkan_menu_smoke` was not run: it opens a native window, and the example only gains the default argument. One machine/GPU; no hydra-toon host frames, swapchain presentation of a premultiplied image, performance or other-platform claim.

- Method/scope: documentation-only source review on base `e23ddae90be121947462efab7d98fa9146dce770`. Read the [panel/data](../../examples/renderer-diagnostics/panel.hpp), [composition](../../examples/renderer-diagnostics/panel.cpp), [host declaration](../../examples/renderer-diagnostics/host.hpp), [host implementation](../../examples/renderer-diagnostics/host.cpp), [copied main](../../examples/renderer-diagnostics/main.cpp), [guide](../guides/renderer-diagnostics.md), [fixture](../../tests/integrations/renderer_diagnostics_tests.cpp), [Settings choice composition](../../examples/settings-starter/main.cpp), [authoring API](../../include/tessera/ui/authoring.hpp), [command schema/resolution](../../src/commands/commands.cpp), [executor](../../src/commands/execution.cpp) and [copy driver](../../tests/cmake/run-consumer.cmake), against the UI-model, commands and input contracts. - Selection/rationale: MSAA adds a discrete choice and dynamic supported membership beside the accepted numeric setting. Settings demonstrates repeated stable named Buttons with a selected marker; panel Rows/Columns and ordinary command eligibility supply the composition boundary. Compared with a Boolean toggle, this operation also exposes capability consistency, unsupported choices and equal-value request accounting without requiring another numeric field or a popup. API sufficiency is a source-based inference, not a compiled result or an executed blocker. The [consumer policy](../integrations/renderer-diagnostics.md#implemented-msaa-choice-boundary) owns exact values, identity, retention and rejection rules; [current](../roadmap/current.md#current-slice) owns the bounded implementation acceptance.
- Settings/command trace: the host owns only `Numeric` accepted/draft state and its eligibility; it has no MSAA/capability observation. `stage` derives command states, applies eligibility before layout/inspection/paint, and retains the old view until executor result validation and `accept`. The command schema validates finite bounded numbers and exact variant types, but number bounds do not validate integral sample counts or a changing supported set. Choice activation needs fixed sample mapping, and typed node-free requests need host membership checks plus disclosure/eligibility guards. `recheck` compares the retained command observation identity and live owner before host entry; every accepted capability replacement must publish that same boundary. No command-schema extension was identified.
- Concrete host friction: `Handler::execute` sends remaining setting commands through the direct-strength parsing/adjustment/reset branch, and `accept` counts changes solely by comparing numeric accepted values. Add explicit MSAA handling and value-change accounting without advancing or canonicalizing the numeric draft. `stage` and `accept` recover collapsed-controls focus only for IDs starting with `direct-`; extend that policy to the chosen MSAA owners. Candidate settings/capabilities and complete validation must travel through refresh, eligibility, commands and navigation preparations as owned values. These are consumer connection tasks identified by inspection, not observed runtime failures or grounds for a generic settings/input framework.
- Retention/input trace: host-owned drafts are already passed unchanged through telemetry refresh and disclosure candidates; a pending invalid draft can coexist with an MSAA change if the new branch avoids numeric parsing/reset. The input host routes focused direct editing before navigation, maps raw activation once, claims panel pointer streams across replacement and records consumption separately from returned errors. New choice IDs can use the ordinary pointer/keyboard/gamepad/semantic path, with focused eligible identity retained and collapse recovering the common header. Capability replacement must cancel pending pointer activation while retaining its panel claim; disabled hits and failed commands must not reach the camera. This extension requires scripted assertions, not a new raw-input API.
- Verification plan/limits: extend the existing fixture's stable observations with samples, capabilities and eligibility, and compare request attempts versus accepted changes, including equal values and entered failures. Check exact numeric draft retention, stale/unsupported/hidden/disabled/node-free rejection, capability updates excluding the accepted value, failure/retry and arbitration. The fixture reconstructs accepted documents/offsets for CPU/replay; it does not replay host commands or camera delivery. The driver copies the exact seven reusable project files through both local imports; extend the copied main and guide alongside implementation, then run both configurations and affected boundary checks. This review establishes no MSAA implementation, new capability row, runtime validation, native renderer/device acknowledgement, GPU capture or performance evidence. - Control trace: Checkbox lowers controlled Boolean observations into ordinary activate owners; repeated named Buttons can express MSAA/material choices and reset without popup APIs. Its pointer refresh cancels outstanding activations on replacement. Settings supplies focused editing routing, but neither example establishes the selected camera contract. Consumer work must track a panel's claimed pointer stream until terminal release/cancel across replacement and mark recognized input consumed even on a returned error, without sending a consumed key a second time as logical activation. It exercises the numeric boundary shared by direct/ambient/vector controls with one setting and no renderer side effects. ## Renderer diagnostics source review — 2026-10-17

- Method/scope: documentation-only intake on Tessera base `9f9704fc6c52a3137174826cf0e036071456db09`. Reviewed the user-supplied [hydra-toon viewport](https://github.com/animu-sphere/hydra-toon/tree/cad1032c210644a66bd45abbb59cb968c2341dde/adapters/viewport), especially [overlay.cpp](https://github.com/animu-sphere/hydra-toon/blob/cad1032c210644a66bd45abbb59cb968c2341dde/adapters/viewport/overlay.cpp) and [overlay.hpp](https://github.com/animu-sphere/hydra-toon/blob/cad1032c210644a66bd45abbb59cb968c2341dde/adapters/viewport/overlay.hpp). GitHub `main` resolved to that commit during review; the local source inspection used the same revision. No external source was modified or runtime executed.
- Source observations: the Telemetry window shows scene/device/extent/presentation information, frame time/FPS/p99/history, CPU/GPU/latency timing tables, draw/upload/session counters and explicit missing-timestamp/empty-series states. Controls include MSAA 1/2/4/8, outlines/self-shadow, lighting scalars/vector and material-view choice/reset, playback, material/morph override editing/release and skeleton/avatar diagnostics. The overlay also handles pointer ownership and copies ImGui draw data/textures into the renderer's overlay format. These are observations of the reference, not Tessera capabilities.
- Boundary trace: `OverlayFrame` supplies display observations and borrowed scene/telemetry/statistics inputs; `OverlayControls` returns requested settings/edits to the host. This separation provides a useful consumer shape, but its concrete renderer types, ImGui draw-list translation and texture protocol are not Tessera APIs. The proposed Tessera panel must lower ordinary authored elements into its shared semantics/layout/paint and expose backend-neutral host snapshots and commands. Native host arbitration, renderer operations and resource completion remain outside core.
- Scope adoption: the user's direction explicitly adds renderer diagnostics authoring to the active milestone. [Current](../roadmap/current.md#renderer-diagnostics-reference-panel) owns bounded telemetry/history, representative controls, independent source consumption and release acceptance, sequenced after the section starter. The [consumer design](../integrations/renderer-diagnostics.md) owns data/command/verification requirements; the full external overlay inventory does not become a new widget framework or whole-repository migration task.
- Verification: `powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts/check-docs.ps1` and `git diff --check` passed. Canonical ownership review keeps consumer requirements in the new integration page, active acceptance in current, broader widget candidates in backlog, source findings here and adopted direction in the changelog. Runtime checks are unnecessary for this documentation-only change.
- Evidence limits: source review establishes the workload, not API sufficiency, an executed blocker, Tessera panel support, native renderer integration or performance. No capability row is added. Live hydra-toon migration and complete object editors/scene overlays require separate consumer scope. Runtime, GPU, native input and external builds are not run for this documentation-only change.

## Settings starter section and scroll review — 2026-10-17

- Method/scope: source review on base `9f9704fc6c52a3137174826cf0e036071456db09`, comparing the [starter source](../../examples/settings-starter/main.cpp), [walkthrough](../guides/settings-starter.md), `SectionSettings`, `run_sections` and `run_scrolling` in the [Settings fixture](../../examples/settings/main.cpp), and their [consumer contracts](../integrations/settings-app.md). This is documentation-only scope selection; no runtime execution or new capability validation accompanies the review. The preceding capability rows retain their recorded evidence.
- Composition trace: the starter's `Field`/`Settings` use one session, controlled username/Checkbox values, derived summary, reusable classes and common Apply/result content. `SectionSettings` builds repeated choices with ordinary iteration, explicit `section-<id>` IDs and Button actions, then branches on the selected application string. Only Account/Feature/About content changes; summary, Apply and result remain outside it. `run_sections::stage` composes a fresh Element tree per candidate. The starter instead stores one `const form`, so extending it requires explicit recomposition; `Session::lower` settles values but does not rerun C++ branches under the [form contract](../architecture/ui-model.md#implemented-c-form-authoring).
- State/publication trace: `run_sections` keeps section order/selection, `TextEditState`, draft, saves and result outside branch elements. Its candidate host copies these values, updates observations, stages the complete presentation and accepts only on success; selection neither edits nor saves. Stable author IDs support ordinary focus refresh after replacement. Removal/restoration of the Account view retains the host edit lifetime rather than requiring keyed component mounting. These are consumer responsibilities expressible with existing APIs; this review demonstrates no core/runtime blocker.
- Input adaptation: the starter routes normalized text/editing keys before logical navigation, while `run_sections` explicitly constructs its edit request in the scripted sequence and routes logical events separately. Copying that sequence alone would not demonstrate the starter's focused-input path across conditional branches. The starter's focus comparison assumes `find("username")` succeeds because its form always contains the field. A conditional extension must require a present eligible owner before comparing focus and creating an edit request: absent focus and an absent field are both empty optionals. This is a source-derived adaptation requirement, not an executed failure in the existing unconditional starter. The acceptance must exercise ignored editing input on an absent Account branch as well as edit/select/return through the normalized host entry point.
- Scrolling trace: `run_scrolling` uses the same composition with root overflow and a 720×160 viewport. Its host stores the applied offset under `settings`, maps it to each new tree, clamps via full-tree layout, copies focus for navigation/recovery, calls `scroll_into_view` and stages again before accepting a reveal. Wheel routing preserves focused identity without forcing reveal. Its initial clipped username/Apply and shorter About branch make those policies observable. The starter's preparation has no offset input/reveal path; declaring overflow alone would not connect that constrained operation. Existing [scrolling evidence](#settings-scrolling-evidence--2026-10-16) owns executed results, and per-view replay reconstructs offsets from zero rather than executing the C++ host.
- Onboarding finding/selection: a reader can copy the edit/Apply starter but must extract repeated-choice composition, candidate recomposition and conditional input routing from the larger regression source to add section selection. Extend that same two-file starter with a fixed Account/Feature/About list, preserving its explicit host and owned saves. [Current work](../roadmap/current.md#current-slice) owns the reproducible edit/select/Apply/return/edit/Apply acceptance, keyboard/gamepad reachability, inspection/CPU checks and stop condition. A viewport that fits all branches isolates that operation; constrained scrolling remains a separate onboarding decision. Dynamic list mutation, theme/interaction expansion and a new widget/input/lifecycle framework are not promoted.
- Verification/limits: `powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts/check-docs.ps1` passed for 56 Markdown files and 2,329 local links, including ownership/status guards; `git diff --check` passed. Canonical ownership review keeps findings here, remaining acceptance in current and delivery history in the changelog. No source, public API, subsystem contract or capability row changes are established. Existing section/scroll fixtures remain the implementation evidence; no new executed input, copied-source build, CPU/replay result, native device/window, persistence, real-font/GPU or performance claim follows from source inspection.

- Revision/scope: local changes on base `62ed64b523e2f7f3dc60ee39ede7696c89726e93`, connecting the [Settings fixture](../../examples/settings/main.cpp) to existing overflow, scroll routing, reveal and full-tree APIs. The [consumer boundary](../integrations/settings-app.md#implemented-constrained-scrolling-boundary) owns exact input, offset retention, focus policy, candidate acceptance and replay limits. A constrained baseline proves username is clipped and Apply is below the viewport; existing APIs suffice, with no core API, authoring API, serialized schema or subsystem contract extension.
- Configuration: reused `build-v040-authoring` and the Windows x64/Visual Studio 18 2026/MSVC 19.51.36256.0 configuration from [section evidence](#settings-section-evidence--2026-10-16); tests/examples enabled, fonts/Vulkan disabled, no dependency toolchain or downloads.
- Procedure/result: build Debug and Release with `cmake --build build-v040-authoring --config <configuration> --parallel 4 -- /nodeReuse:false`; run `ctest --test-dir build-v040-authoring -C <configuration> --output-on-failure --no-tests=error --output-log build-v040-authoring/scroll-tests-<configuration-lowercase>.log -j 4`. All 54 checks passed in each configuration, including Settings, scroll/layout/input/paint/inspection/replay contracts, existing examples and both independent source imports. Both full build logs contain no compiler warnings. The focused Debug Settings check also passed before full-suite verification.
- Application observations: 720×160 logical root viewport, 12-point padding/gaps, initial zero offset, root `settings` as sole scroll owner. Wheel +48 at (16,16) applies exactly; four logical focus-next events reveal username, committed `本` yields `Ada本` at 6:6, and focus-next reveals Apply for one gamepad save. Clipped Apply has no hit; revealed pointer release identifies its ordinary activation owner, without executing another save. +4096/-4096 reach the limits. About replacement from Account's bottom clamps the retained offset to its shorter extent while Apply stays revealed. Returning through Account/Feature/About retains edit owner/revision/selection, draft and owned save copies/result; Feature keyboard toggle/Apply saves a second `Ada本` / true copy.
- Boundaries/rejection: every descendant clip and inspection border/clip equals its full-tree geometry. Focus reveal is idempotent after navigation. Wheel input preserves focused identity without forcing reveal; command/branch candidates reveal recovered focus before publication. Non-finite deltas, replaced-tree reveal handles, pending command invocations and disabled Apply focus/pointer/semantic/direct requests reject. Injected measurement and paint failures during scroll and Apply preserve offsets, focus, edit/draft/selection, saved copies/result, accepted document/inspection/command generation and paint, with source-located diagnostics. Entered command sequences reject duplicate execution, and restaging reproduces accepted document/geometry/paint after rejection.
- Capture/replay: 24 accepted-view observations use scale 1.25, locale `en`, placeholder metrics, synthetic glyphs and an explicit tick at 2 microseconds. Each accepted document/style view reconstructs its applied offset from zero using normalized scroll at 1 microsecond when needed. Two runs reproduce geometry, semantic records, paint and independently captured CPU RGBA8 bytes exactly. This is per-view replay; it does not serialize host offset retention or re-execute the C++ navigation/edit/save sequence. The pre-existing section, Apply, username and Checkbox recording checks also pass.
- Artifacts/environment: `scroll-focused-debug.log`, `scroll-build-debug.log`, `scroll-build-release.log`, `scroll-tests-debug.log`, `scroll-tests-release.log` and executables under ignored `build-v040-authoring`; independent import logs remain in configuration-specific consumer directories. Images stay in memory. The sandboxed whole Debug build stopped producing output and was interrupted; normal toolchain execution completed both full builds and full suites. Documentation/link/ownership and whitespace checks accompany the change.
- Limits: single-owner scripted scrolling, one session and synchronous in-memory save. No ScrollView convenience, generalized scroll-state framework, native input/window, real-font/GPU Settings capture, persistence, new editing behavior, general rollback, other-platform or performance claim. [Current](../roadmap/current.md) selects reusable-style review as the next consumer operation.

- Application observations: Account starts selected in `account, feature, about` order. Committed text edits `Ada` to `Ada本` at 6:6, keyboard selection shows Feature, Checkbox toggles true and Apply records one owned `Ada本` / true copy. Gamepad logical activation selects About, pointer release selects Account and semantic activation selects Feature; press alone does nothing. Selection preserves draft/edit and save copies/result. - Identity/retention: after returning to Account, reorder to `about, account, feature` and unselected About removal retain exact username owner/revision/selection and focus. Selected Account removal chooses Feature and recovers focus to Checkbox by surviving-neighbor rules. Restoring all choices retains Checkbox focus; selecting Account restores username focus and its exact host edit observation. - Rejection: empty/duplicate/unknown lists and absent selected IDs return located candidate errors without changing accepted selection/order, draft/edit, saves/result, document/inspection, command generation or paint. Authored-disabled and command-disabled choices deny focus/logical, pointer, semantic and direct action resolution. Pending invocations, old section owners, an old username owner and a foreign tree with equal command counters reject without host entry. Restaging after rejection reproduces accepted semantic observations.

Sixteen in-memory CPU RGBA8 frames reproduce exactly across two runs. All fifteen accepted presentations match replay geometry, semantic names, paint and independently captured CPU bytes; nine ordinary replay actions reproduce seven section selections, one toggle and one Apply. Host-translated text and list replacements are accepted views; replay does not execute the section/save host. Existing Apply, username and Checkbox variants also pass.

Images stay in memory. The sandboxed whole Debug build stopped producing output and was interrupted before compilation; normal toolchain execution completed both builds and full test runs. Documentation/link/ownership and whitespace checks accompany the change.
- Limits: closed three-section scripted consumer, host-owned state in one session, synchronous in-memory save and synthetic inputs/capture. No per-section local-state lifetime, dynamic mounting, scrolling, new editing behavior, native host, installed-font/GPU Settings capture, filesystem save, generalized rollback or performance claim. No batching, device loss, real fonts, keyboard/gamepad input, deployment, or performance claim. There are no declared state slots, raw-input injection, waiting predicates, render counters, image capture, or diagnostics envelope. Determinism is checked by repeated capture on this toolchain only. The Win32 menu smoke was not run for this slice.

## Overflow clip evidence — 2026-10-05

- Revision: uncommitted implementation on base `fc0ec66` (`fc0ec661afa27fe6bb5b9d07169385ca862f1e7a`). Identifiable source adds `Overflow` to [resolved_style.hpp](../../include/tessera/style/resolved_style.hpp), `intersect` to [geometry.hpp](../../include/tessera/layout/geometry.hpp), and `LayoutBox::clip` to [layout_box.hpp](../../include/tessera/layout/layout_box.hpp); records and validates clips in `src/layout/flex_layout.cpp`, `src/layout/layout_snapshot.cpp`, and `src/detail/layout_snapshot.hpp`; consumes them in `src/render/paint.cpp`, `src/input/pointer.cpp`, `src/inspection/inspection.cpp`, and `src/replay/replay.cpp`; changes scissor rounding in the [Vulkan renderer](../../backends/vulkan/renderer.cpp); adds the clipped list to the [menu host](../../examples/vulkan-menu/main.cpp); and extends layout, paint, pointer, inspection, replay, and GPU checks.
- Source fingerprint: SHA-256 `8c50cc29d7c0a12c861c4d4c53ec70c605cdf722032516a3f479547c60c7b0e1`, computed with the foundation procedure over `CMakeLists.txt`, `include`, `src`, `examples`, `tests`, and `backends` (61 files). Documentation is outside this fingerprint.
- Configuration: Windows NT `10.0.26200.0` x64, MSVC `19.51.36256.0`, Visual Studio 18 2026, CMake/CTest `4.4.3`, Debug; existing `build` (Vulkan disabled) and `build-vulkan` (Vulkan SDK `1.4.350.0`, Slang `2026.8`) configurations. No dependency download or vendoring occurred.
- GPU/runtime: NVIDIA RTX A5000 (`vendorID=0x10de`, `deviceID=0x2231`), raw driver `2504261632`, device Vulkan `1.4.329`, Khronos validation with synchronization validation. Native host `B8G8R8A8_SRGB`, FIFO, two frames in flight, initial 96 DPI.
- Procedure: the [development](../guides/development.md) core build/CTest in `build`; [optional Vulkan build/CTest and four SPIR-V checks](../guides/development.md#optional-vulkan-workflow); direct `tessera_vulkan_menu.exe --smoke`; fresh `build-clip-core` configure/build with Vulkan/tests/examples disabled.
- Result: all fifteen core CTest targets and all seventeen `build-vulkan` targets passed, including `vulkan` and `vulkan_menu_smoke`, with no validation errors; all builds produced no warnings, and all four shaders passed `spirv-val --target-env vulkan1.1`. Numeric checks show recorded clips equal to the clipping ancestor's padding box, nested intersection, no clip on the clipping box itself, paint push/pop runs that share one pair across adjacent boxes, `layout_clip` rejection of stale or non-finite clips, half-open clipped targeting with fall-through, no activation from a clipped press or release, clipped inspection point resolution, and replay clip comparison. The offscreen fractional mirrored scissor fixture matched the pixel-center edges at scales 1/1.25/1.5/2.
- Native result: the clipped list made the menu two batches (32 primitives, 3584 payload upload bytes per frame). At 480x360 (scale 1), 726x557 (1.5), and 603x459 (1.25), every scanned pixel within three rows of the list clip edge showed the straddling Credits button exactly when `hit_test` at its pixel center targeted it. The clip edge falls at physical 274.15 and 347.19 at scales 1 and 1.25, where outward rounding would paint an unhittable row. A posted click on the first row outside the edge produced no action, and a click on the last row inside produced `show-credits`. The action sequence was `start-game`, `start-game`, `show-credits`, `quit-game`; 21 frames were presented and retired. With outward scissor rounding temporarily restored, the smoke failed at pixel 141,274 in the first capture; the pixel-center rule was then reinstated and every check rerun.
- Artifacts: `build/Testing/Temporary/LastTest.log`, `build-vulkan/Testing/Temporary/LastTest.log`, `build-vulkan/backends/vulkan/artifacts/scale-{4,5,6,8}.ppm`, `vulkan-menu-{1,2,3}.ppm`, and `build-clip-core/Debug/tessera_core.lib`. These are local ignored outputs reproduced by the source.
- Limits: rectangular, axis-aligned clips only. There are no scroll offsets or extents, no transforms in generated paint or hit testing, no corner-radius clipping, and no viewport clip. The clip is set through host-supplied resolved styles, with no JSON v1 or stylesheet syntax. Native agreement was checked with posted messages on one device and Debug only. It is not physical-device input or real monitor DPI evidence. Semantic projection does not exclude clipped-out nodes.

Layout checks show a scroll extent covering children's margin boxes plus trailing padding, per-axis clamping of requested offsets, children moved back by the offset under a fixed viewport clip, nested composition and clip intersection, ignored offsets on non-scroll boxes, and located `scroll_count`, `invalid_number`, and `non_finite_geometry` failures. Replay settled a generation for a scroll step and attributed a later click to the moved button. Separately, dropping the clip from hit testing, the position carry from reveal, or chaining after the first scroll box each made the scroll check fail; each change was reverted. The first smoke run failed at step 18: the revealed list offset `0x1.7b332p+4` was one `float` step below the limit `0x1.7b333p+4`, because the fractional list height rounds differently in the two subtractions. The smoke now compares that offset within 1e-4 logical units, the layout numerics tolerance. The smoke then passed its existing steps unchanged with no validation-layer messages. At scale 1.25, D-pad focus scrolled Credits fully into view, a quarter-notch posted wheel moved the list back exactly 10 logical units, the fifth capture matched paint and `hit_test` within three rows of both the bottom (Credits) and top (Start) clip edges, a click on the scrolled Credits requested `show-credits`, and a full notch over the title left the offset unchanged. The action sequence was `start-game`, `start-game`, `show-credits`, `start-game`, `show-credits`, `show-credits`, `quit-game`; 49 frames were presented and retired. Disabling the host's reveal call made smoke step 18 fail; the change was reverted.
- Artifacts: `build/Testing/Temporary/LastTest.log`, `build-vulkan/Testing/Temporary/LastTest.log`, `build-vulkan/backends/vulkan/artifacts/vulkan-menu-{1,2,3,4,5}.ppm`, and `build-scroll-core/Debug/tessera_core.lib` are local ignored outputs reproduced by the source.
- Limits: in-process routing and host-built stylesheet declarations only; JSON v1 has no overflow field. Wheel messages were posted, not produced by a physical wheel, and `WM_MOUSEHWHEEL`, horizontal native scrolling, and Shift-wheel conventions were not exercised; horizontal offsets are checked numerically in-process only. Extents ignore grandchild overflow. There is no scroll anchoring, virtualization, smooth scrolling, keyboard page scrolling, or wheel latching, and focus dispatch itself never scrolls. Semantic projection neither excludes scrolled-out nodes nor exposes scroll actions. One machine and GPU, Debug only.

After `Navigate` focused Quit, a reload that kept the `quit` ID restored focus to it, and a reload that disabled Quit recovered focus to Resume, projected Quit as disabled, non-focusable, and without actions, and a following `Activate` requested `start-game` from Resume. An unlabeled button produced `missing_name` warnings at `/nodes/1` and `/steps/0/nodes/1` with the output. Comparison located focus, entry, entry-count, and observation-count differences, and command steps shared the pointer/scroll timestamp stream in both directions. Separately, clearing focus at settled generations, skipping the observation after commands, and skipping semantic comparison each made the replay check fail (as did leaving a stale focus handle unrefreshed, which rejected the replay); each change was reverted.

Focus dispatch does not scroll, so command steps do not reveal focused nodes in scroll boxes. Keys are rejected rather than translated. Determinism is checked by repeated playback on this toolchain only. The Vulkan backend and Win32 host were not rebuilt for this slice. One machine, Debug only.

A replay fixture in a 60-unit scrolled menu reveals Quit after a reload restores it by ID and Options after a reload removes Quit, each in one generation per reload whose semantics report the revealed focus; without the policy both reloads keep offset 0. Separately, clearing focus instead of recovering beside the surviving predecessor, and settling a reload without reveal, each made the corresponding check fail; both changes were reverted.

- Limits: in-process synthetic replay and focus fixtures only. Recovery positions come from focusable nodes' author IDs when focus was last stored; there are no keyed component identities, scroll anchoring, or preserved offsets across reload. Resize and scroll steps do not reveal focus, and the Win32 host performs no reload, so neither native smoke exercises this path. Offscreen runner frames were not captured for the new fixture; the runner captures generations as replay settles them. Release, fonts, and device/driver details were not re-recorded. One machine/toolchain; no physical-controller evidence.

The native menu's list has a fixed height inside a fixed-width panel, so its resize and DPI steps never hide focus and neither native smoke exercises the new host path. Callable/comparator errors, direct/indirect cycles, attempted derivation writes, reentrant flush, cross-runtime/thread entry, and failed candidate retries return located diagnostics. Name/UTF-8 validation, live-node budget reclamation, nesting bounds, and depth-error recovery are exercised.
- Boundary correction: initial recursive-depth fixtures exceeded the default Windows Debug stack before the diagnostic at larger limits. The delivered owner/batch/evaluation limit is 32; its overflow fixture returns `reactive_depth` and subsequently settles through an ordered flush in both configurations. This is a bounded graph claim, not arbitrary recursion support.
- Artifacts: `build-v030-reactive/reactive-{debug,release}.log`, `Debug`/`Release` test and example executables, and `tessera_core.lib` in the same build tree are ignored local outputs. No image baseline or GPU checks.
- Limits: one machine/toolchain, synchronous copyable C++ values/callables, explicit host flush, and eager initialization of all live calculations during flush. Graph caches are not atomic UI publication or application rollback. No effect delivery/cleanup registration, async executor, component/reconciliation/binding API, graph inspection/recording schema, incremental presentation, native host integration, CPU renderer, transport/CLI, downstream/install/export package, or performance claim. UI/Replay/font serialization and dependency versions are unchanged.

The tool-panel subscription is a synthetic host flag. The host must explicitly dispose and deliver shutdown cleanup; omitted delivery releases callback captures without invoking external operations. Callable failure cannot roll back external side effects and is not retried. One machine/toolchain; no new platform, fonts, GPU, physical-device, packaging, or performance claim. Serialized formats and dependency versions are unchanged.

- Boundary evidence: settlement alone and batch-internal explicit reads deliver nothing; one batch publishes its final values once. Phases deliver independently in effect creation order; republishing unchanged or equal effect outputs captures nothing; an undelivered capture is replaced by a later publication. Unflushed writes, open batches, and a flush that fails after effect calculations refreshed reject publication, and no effect observes the rejected candidate; a later successful publication delivers only the latest value. Replacement runs the prior cleanup once before applying; owner disposal discards pending deliveries, stops effect calculation, and queues the latest cleanup once in registration order with ordinary cleanup registrations. Standard/nonstandard application failures and a failed replacement cleanup return ordered located diagnostics while later effects run and are not retried; allocation failure propagates and leaves only undelivered effects queued. Effects can read settled values and write sources; flush, publication, recursive delivery, cleanup delivery, batches, disposal, and registration are rejected during delivery, writes in effect calculations are rejected, and background-thread delivery fails. Converging feedback drains in seven publications; unbounded feedback fails at the writing effect after 32 feedback rounds and publication resumes after its owner is disposed. Effect slots share the 4,096 live-plus-pending cleanup budget, retain it while queued, and release it on delivery. Foreign owners, undeclared phases, and duplicate effect names are rejected; effect, value, and cleanup names are separate namespaces.
- Artifacts: `build-v030-effects/effects-{debug,release}.log`, Debug/Release tests/examples, and `tessera_core.lib` are ignored local outputs. No new image baseline or GPU checks.
- Limits: synchronous copyable values/callables on one host UI thread with explicit host flush, publication, and delivery. Publication covers the whole graph, so an unrelated failed calculation blocks every effect; there is no subtree error fallback, presentation generation identity, effect result adapter, async executor, GPU-completion phase, component/binding API, or native-host integration. The tool-panel status line is a synthetic host string. Failed external operations cannot roll back side effects and are not retried. - Boundary evidence: a failed prerequisite shared by consumers in its own and a sibling boundary calculates once per flush, faults both boundaries and nested descendants, and is returned once with its escaped location. Unheld effects publish and deliver while held effects keep their last values; stale held calculations do not block publication, but a stale calculation outside faulted boundaries does. A later flush retries, clears recovered boundaries, and delivers their latest values in creation order. A root-region consumer of a contained failure escapes, stops flush, and blocks publication until its owner is disposed. A root consumer that abandons a prerequisite failed earlier in the same flush leaves that failure contained without recalculation. Allocation failure inside a boundary propagates. Disposed, foreign, tracked-calculation fault queries and duplicate boundary names are rejected; disposing faulted boundaries releases held state. Existing graph, cleanup, effect, and feedback checks pass unchanged.
- Artifacts: `build-v030-boundaries/boundaries-{debug,release}.log`, Debug/Release tests/examples, and `tessera_core.lib` are ignored local outputs. No new image baseline or GPU checks.
- Limits: in-process containment and located diagnostics on one host UI thread with explicit host flush, fault queries, publication, and delivery. No fallback content, UI error boundary or component mapping, presentation generation identity, reload transaction, retry policy, async executor, or native-host integration. The tool-panel record table and fallback text are synthetic host fixtures. One machine/toolchain; no new platform, fonts, GPU, physical-device, packaging, or performance claim. Serialized formats and dependency versions are unchanged.

Its inventory then publishes four keyed generations: a host reorder keeps the pinned Lantern row's lifetime, local state, and focus with one label recalculation caused only by the pin; removal delivers that row's thumbnail cleanup and focus recovers to a neighbor; reinsertion creates a new unpinned lifetime and rejects the old lifetime as stale; a prop change recalculates only its row label. Shutdown delivers the remaining row cleanups through the panel owner.
- Reconciliation evidence: reorder neither creates nor disposes instances, keeps local state revisions, recalculates no binding, and keeps subscriptions live for a later prop change. Removal and incompatible kind replacement report disposed instances in previous order, queue each cleanup once for host delivery, fail later reads of disposed values, and start replacements and reused keys with fresh lifetimes and state; an empty candidate disposes every instance. Empty, control-character, invalid-UTF-8, over-length, and duplicate keys, empty kinds, and over-limit candidates return located diagnostics without changing instances, queuing cleanup, or consuming lifetimes. Foreign-runtime and calculation-time creation leave the collection unchanged; a replacement-only foreign-runtime failure drops the instances it disposed; an owner-budget failure keeps previous instances and releases owners created by that call; a disposed scope rejects new keys.
- Artifacts: `build-v030-keyed/keyed-{debug,release}.log`, Debug/Release tests/examples, and `tessera_core.lib` are ignored local outputs. No new image baseline or GPU checks.
- Limits: in-process reconciliation of one flat sibling collection on one host UI thread, with host-built nodes, host-registered instance state, and full-tree style/layout/semantic/paint evaluation. No typed presentation binding, component description or slot API, serialized key, unkeyed matching, cross-parent move, virtualization, incremental stage update, UI error boundary, async resource retirement, or native-host integration. Focus follows rows only because the host uses keys as author IDs. The tool-panel items, pin toggle, and thumbnail subscription are synthetic host fixtures. One machine/toolchain; no new platform, fonts, GPU, physical-device, packaging, or performance claim. Serialized formats and dependency versions are unchanged.

Changed-property stage unions come from reflection, compare against the supplied document, and report no stages for equal values. Healthy bindings can compose a document beside a faulted boundary; binding a failed calculation or even a clean source under that boundary rejects without retry. Foreign, expired and disposed references fail at document property locations; keyed removal/reinsertion with the same author ID cannot revive the old binding.
- Artifacts: `build-v030-bindings/bindings-{debug,release}.log`, Debug/Release tests/examples, and `tessera_core.lib` are ignored local outputs. No image baseline or GPU checks.

The host owns typed prop sources, local state, candidate acceptance, fallback composition and publication; there is no component description/slot schema, optional-property removal, style/event binding, incremental invalidation, automatic last-valid UI store, reload transaction, async resource retirement or native-host integration. - Boundary evidence: a host-owned inventory fixture preserves one opening record/token across three command generations and three coherent reactive publications, then commits exactly one history entry. Non-editing requests create no transaction and do not join an active edit; both execution paths share request deduplication. Pointer, keyboard, gamepad, accessibility, agent and replay source records use equal typed edits and closure accounting. A serialized ordinary UI recording deterministically yields four pointer/navigation/semantic action observations in two playbacks; each resolves through the current semantic owner and produces one discrete begin/edit/commit pair using host fixture schemas, with no new recording format.

Host command errors, cancellation, malformed success fields and exceptions trigger one rollback; rollback failure is reported alongside command diagnostics without a restoration claim. Stale begin enters no host. Stale dispatched execution, revoked affected IDs, disabled commands, replaced registry/tree, absent owner snapshots and mismatched gesture owners invalidate before edit/commit and roll back. Empty edits cannot create history. Nested begin, wrong thread, recursive host entry, invalid/oversized/non-UTF-8 metadata, invalid reasons, foreign session/host and closed tokens reject with located diagnostics. Entered failed begin cannot retry its opening sequence. Boundary errors, malformed success values, cancellation and exceptions produce no token or consume closure once. Session destruction expires weak tokens without callbacks. An entered allocation failure consumes execution but leaves the token open for explicit rollback recovery.

The host owns mutation/history, atomic begin failure, session/host lifetimes, coherent observations and post-dispatch calls; input completion/cancellation, capture loss and reload/disposal require explicit host wiring. Commit/rollback failure closes the token without retry or an automatic second boundary, and does not prove host state restoration. ## Scalar CPU rendering evidence — 2026-10-12

- Revision: working-tree scalar renderer/capture implementation in [cpu_renderer.hpp](../../include/tessera/render/cpu_renderer.hpp) and [cpu_renderer.cpp](../../src/render/cpu_renderer.cpp), [CPU fixtures](../../tests/render/cpu_renderer_tests.cpp), the [source consumer](../../tests/cmake/consumer/main.cpp), and [Vulkan comparison fixtures](../../tests/render/vulkan_tests.cpp). - Configuration: Windows x64, Visual Studio 18 2026, MSVC 19.51.36256.0 and Windows SDK 10.0.26100.0. `build-v030-cpu` uses optional Vulkan/fonts OFF; `build-v030-cpu-vulkan` uses Vulkan ON, examples/fonts OFF, Vulkan SDK 1.4.350.0 and Slang 2026.8. GPU: NVIDIA RTX A5000, vendor 4318, driver value 2504261632, Vulkan 1.4.329, RGBA8 sRGB target, required Khronos validation and synchronization validation. CPU paths require no window/device or optional font/GPU library.
- Procedure: configure/build under [core](../guides/development.md#core-build-workflow), [scalar capture](../guides/development.md#scalar-cpu-capture-workflow), and [optional Vulkan](../guides/development.md#optional-vulkan-workflow), with the directories/options above. Core checks use `ctest --test-dir build-v030-cpu -C Debug --output-on-failure` and the Release equivalent. GPU checks build target `tessera_vulkan_tests`, then use `ctest --test-dir build-v030-cpu-vulkan -C Debug -R '^vulkan$' --output-on-failure` and Release. Documentation checks use `powershell -NoProfile -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File scripts/check-docs.ps1`; whitespace checks use `git diff --check`.
- Boundary evidence: ordered fills/alpha, circular rounded corners, unequal and collapsed inside borders, nested fractional/mirrored clips, transform composition/restoration, rotation/skew and framebuffer top/left edge inclusion at pixel centers, fractional scale and outward extent. Image fixtures check copied lifetime, sRGB decoding before bilinear filtering, UNORM linear data, crop/tint/alpha, capacity, clipped-image retirement and future-retire clamping. Invalid stacks, transformed overflow, missing images, invalid crops (including zero-area commands), bytes/encoding/extents and stale frames reject without changing pixels, resource use or frame sequence. Synthetic grayscale cache fixtures verify baseline/coverage, repeated capture, host/atlas handle separation, missing-cache failure, located raster failures, recursive capture/binding rejection and partial atlas-binding rollback at shared capacity. Offscreen replay captures resize/scale generations repeatedly; independent source hosts exercise public CPU capture through both import modes.
- GPU comparison: both reference and adjacent batch paths render one 32x32 logical mixed primitive/image/synthetic-grayscale-glyph scene at scales 1, 1.25, 1.5 and 2, against repeated CPU captures. All RGBA values are compared after sRGB RGB decode with alpha linear, at maximum absolute tolerance 2/255. Maximum errors by scale are 0.00736163, 0.00658295, 0.00705586 and 0.00628694 in both paths and configurations. Each GPU check passed with zero validation errors. Inner-border discard edges and bitmap quad edges avoid exact pixel centers in this comparison; GPU interpolation and subpixel quantization can change coverage at those boundaries. CPU edge fixtures separately exercise exact transformed pixel-center coverage.
- Result: all 40 core CTest targets passed in Debug and Release, including both independent source consumers exercising public scalar capture. The optional `vulkan` check passed in both configurations, including all eight CPU/GPU comparisons and the existing primitive/image/placeholder/runner fixtures. Documentation verification passed for 33 Markdown files and 1,555 local links with ownership/status guards; `git diff --check` passed.
- Artifacts and limits: the final Release CTest log is preserved as `build-v030-cpu/cpu-release-tests.log`; per-configuration source-consumer logs, binaries and CTest logs are ignored local outputs. GPU artifacts are `build-v030-cpu-vulkan/backends/vulkan/artifacts/cpu-compare-{reference,batch}-{1,2,3,4}.ppm`, and its Release CTest log is preserved as `build-v030-cpu-vulkan/cpu-release-gpu-tests.log`. One machine/toolchain/GPU and synthetic rasters only; no real-font CPU profile, other-platform bit identity, native presentation, broad arbitrary-edge GPU equivalence, performance, worker-thread/SIMD, image encoding, snapshot bundle or CLI claim. CPU capture clears to transparent, while direct rendering borrows and composites over host target bytes. The host chooses assets/rasterizers explicitly; direct glyph commands require atlas lowering and no placeholder/font substitution occurs.

- Boundary fixtures: all four exact state types, declaration/key reorder, changed initializers, named slot compatibility, entire-candidate rejection before runtime entry, null descriptions, escaped access paths, invalid names/types/UTF-8/numbers/references, exact and excessive text/name/state/collection bounds, fresh key lifetimes, once-only ordered host cleanup, queued effect invalidation and late async completion rejection. Live-value exhaustion tests release initialized siblings and failed replacement owners while retaining compatible owners, then retry with fresh state. Nested batches, foreign runtimes, UI-thread checks and calculation/effect entry rejection cover compatible reorders as well as creation. Ordinary single/nested lowering validates declarations, serialization omits runtime state, and full-tree style/layout/semantic/paint/owned source observations accept settled registered values. Both independent CMake import modes consume the public mounting boundary; the tool panel registers its pinned state automatically.
- Result: all 45 CTest checks passed in Debug and Release on the final sources, including the tool panel and both independent source consumers. Final builds produced no compiler warnings. Documentation verification passed for 33 Markdown files and 1,712 local links with ownership/status guards; `git diff --check` passed. Incremental final builds are recorded in `{Debug,Release}-final-build.log`. - Artifacts: `build-v030-component-mount/{Debug,Release}-ctest.log`, configuration-specific build logs, CTest logs, libraries/binaries and source-consumer phase logs are ignored local outputs.
- Limits: one explicitly reconciled sibling collection; mounting registers only kind/local-state schema, while the host validates and accepts ordinary full-tree presentation separately. Same-kind state schema changes reject; kind replacement recreates the whole owner. State initialization uses bounded literals, and subsequent writes use ordinary signal semantics. No automatic invocation-tree setup, declared output signals, component effect/async registration, automatic cancellation/detachment, UI fallback, state declaration/default source spans, state inspection encoding/injection, atomic reload/migration, resource payloads/retirement, optional GPU/font/native, other-platform or performance claim. Serialized formats, dependency pins and release metadata are unchanged.

CTest logs, configuration-specific consumer phase logs and binaries are ignored local outputs.
- Limits: one explicitly reconciled sibling collection with synchronous host registration callbacks; mounting and full presentation acceptance are separate boundaries. Same-kind schema compatibility is unchanged; a reused lifetime does not rerun a changed callback. Cleanup queues and reactive budgets are ordinary runtime contracts, and callback side effects or writes to preexisting owners are not rolled back. No automatic invocation-tree mounting, declared output signals, automatic execution/cancellation/detachment, UI fallback, declaration/default source origins, atomic reload/migration, resource payloads/retirement, optional GPU/font/native, other-platform or performance claim. - Allocation fixtures: one-shot failures scan registration and begin/progress/complete/fail/cancel transitions, comparing cleanup against the accepted source after each failure. Each transition fixture requires a failure after source acceptance, proving that a failed invalidation walk cannot leave the queued detachment address obsolete. The initial unrestricted Debug fixture timed out inside that bookkeeping path; the final fixture scopes injection to exceptions that can reach the tested API.

The initial async fixture's expected host-failure location was corrected to the ordinary `/owners/<name>/cleanups/<name>` path.

- Limits: opt-in owner-disposal registration from host setup, one active attempt per primitive observation, explicit host delivery and ordinary cleanup limits. Supersession and explicit cancellation keep their host detachment boundary. No execution provider, declared component signal/effect/async schemas, declaration-origin diagnostics, UI fallback, atomic reload/migration, resource payloads/GPU retirement, optional GPU/font/native, other-platform or performance claim. ## Recording future evidence

Each claim needs a revision, date, OS/compiler/build configuration, dependency versions, command or procedure, fixture, result/artifact location, and known limitations. GPU claims also need device/driver/backend/target-format information. Separate shader compilation, headless rendering, native-window behavior, installation/consumer checks, and performance measurements.

Update only the rows proven by the delivered work: add a row when a capability first gains implementation evidence, keep each row to its status, scope, and evidence links, and put details and limits in a new dated section. Milestone labels alone do not establish capability. Configurations without evidence have no row. Active work is in [current](../roadmap/current.md); planned scope is in [backlog](../roadmap/backlog.md).
